v1.0.7b — 2026-05-29 — lb-sidecar SOT alignment (G1–G6)

AspectWhat changed / mergedResolved by
G1 — Anomaly exclusion survives partial RL rankings SOT §3.4 line 1756 / §16 line 3699: "Policy safe_mode can short-circuit RL but never bypasses anomaly exclusion." Previously, when RL published weights for only the eligible subset (PPO's is_eligible filter omits unhealthy/unknown backends), the adapter's set_upstream_weights replaced the full weights dict — the previously-excluded backend disappeared entirely from upstream.conf instead of staying as server … down;. Anomaly exclusion was silently bypassed by every RL publish. handle_routing now merges the RL-supplied weights with the full known-backend pool (starting from a floor weight of 1 for every known backend, then overlaying RL's values) so excluded backends always appear in upstream.conf and the adapter renders them as down; per the existing exclusion set. This release
G2 — backend_health hydration on startup SOT §30 lines 7635 + 7760: "LB sidecar consumes BACKEND_HEALTH_QUERY." The sidecar previously relied solely on smartload.anomaly pub/sub for exclusion state — a Redis disconnect or container restart wiped the in-memory exclusion set until anomaly-detector republished. _hydrate_excluded_from_db now runs once after adapter init: reads the latest backend_health row per backend within a configurable window (default 5 min), and calls adapter.exclude_backend for every status=unhealthy entry. Failure is non-fatal — a missing DB, empty result set, or connect error degrade gracefully to "no hydration" and the loop continues. This release
G3 — rl_confidence_threshold gate SOT §13 line 3128: "rl_confidence_threshold: 0.6 # below this, sidecar ignores RL and uses classical." New PolicyState dataclass in runloop.py tracks safe_mode and rl_confidence_threshold across smartload.policy publishes. handle_routing accepts confidence_threshold as a kwarg; treats confidence = max(scores) (matches the argmax-dominant serving shape from v1.0.7); when confidence is below threshold, rejects the recommendation and the previous adapter state stands. Threshold of 0 disables the gate (default before any policy publish). Smoke-verified by raising threshold to 0.9 → sidecar logs routing rejected (confidence=0.700 < threshold=0.900); using previous weights with PPO's 0.7 argmax-dominant confidence. This release
G4 — Shadow envelopes logged SOT v1.0.6 row line 5703: "shadow envelopes received but not applied." Code previously had pass # shadow mode: log nothing extra — operators had no way to confirm shadow envelopes were being received. The drain loop now emits routing shadow — not applied (N rankings) and surfaces confidence on every RoutingOutcome. This release
G5 — Graceful shutdown Module-level _shutdown_event + SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers installed from __main__. The run loop polls the event between message reads; on shutdown closes pubsub and prints run loop exited. Docker SIGTERM during compose down now produces a clean exit instead of leaving an orphan pubsub subscription on Redis. Matches the symmetric L4 fix from v1.0.7 in the RL engine. This release
G6 — Redis reconnect SOT §8.1 line 2299: "If Redis is unreachable or the sidecar dies: NGINX continues serving traffic with the last successfully applied config." NGINX-side guarantee was already correct; the sidecar previously crashed its drain loop on redis.exceptions.ConnectionError and stopped processing forever. The loop now catches it, closes the dead pubsub, sleeps _REDIS_RECONNECT_BACKOFF_SECONDS (default 2 s), and rebuilds the subscription. NGINX keeps serving the last applied weights throughout. This release
/health surface — new fields policy_safe_mode and rl_confidence_threshold added to the runloop-enabled /health body so operators can confirm the policy snapshot the sidecar is reasoning under without grepping container logs. This release
Test coverage tests/unit/lb-sidecar/test_runloop.py grew from 35 → 45 cases. New: partial-ranking merge with floor weights (G1), confidence-threshold rejection above + below (G3), confidence reporting on shadow envelopes (G4), case-insensitive active mode, PolicyState mutation across publishes including partial-publish and malformed-threshold fallback. This release

v1.0.7 — 2026-05-28 — RL review fixes (C1–C5, M1–M10, L4)

AspectWhat changed / mergedResolved by
Contextual-bandit reframe (C1, M9) The offline simulator (services/rl-engine/training/simulator.py) replays Alibaba trace windows independently of the agent's action — there are no environment dynamics. Module docstrings + READMEs now explicitly frame the trained policy as a contextual bandit trained with MaskablePPO on logged production traces, not an MDP-trained RL agent. The closed-loop "consequence" axis lives in the deterministic safety machinery (NGINX max_fails, anomaly-detector exclusions, autoscaler reactivity). Imbalance term in reward.py is now documented as a regulariser over the trace's historical spread, not a learned consequence of weighting. This release
Argmax-dominant serving weights (C2) PPOPolicy.act previously took a single forward pass for argmax then a second one to extract logits, then softmaxed them into per-backend weights. That distribution was never an optimisation target — Discrete(N) PPO learns argmax, not calibrated mass. Replaced with a single forward pass that produces argmax + ranking together: chosen backend receives _DOMINANT_WEIGHT (0.7); the remaining 0.3 is split evenly across other eligibles. Matches what the policy was trained for and keeps a floor so NGINX health probing of the rest still works. This release
mean_eval_reward → mean_training_reward (C3a) The shipped artifact_meta.json field was sourced from model.ep_info_buffer (training rollouts), not from eval_harness against the held-out seed bank — the old name overclaimed. Renamed in train_ppo.py and the committed artifact. To populate a real held-out number, run training/eval_harness.py on policy.zip and store the result alongside. This release
PPO reload without disk re-read (C4) _refresh_policy_under_lock previously called bootstrap_policy on every smartload.policy publish, which reconstructed PPOPolicy → reloaded policy.zip from disk → rebuilt the torch graph. Now RoutingPolicy.reload(**kwargs) is a real in-place update hook: PPOPolicy.reload overrides it to update _operating_mode / _confidence_threshold / _exploration_rate without touching the artifact. The refresh path falls back to full bootstrap only when reload raises NotImplementedError or TypeError. This release
Anomaly-health TTL eviction (C5) _anomaly_health in app.py previously grew without bound — a backend ID seen once on smartload.anomaly stayed in the dict forever. Each entry now carries a monotonic seen-at timestamp; _evict_stale_anomaly_health runs at the top of every inference cycle and drops entries older than ANOMALY_HEALTH_TTL_MULTIPLIER × RL_WINDOW_SECONDS (default 6 × 30 s = 3 min). Keeps the dict bounded under backend churn. This release
"unknown" health state (M1) A backend with NULL latency and NULL error_rate in RL_STATE_QUERY was previously coerced to health=healthy — silence read as a positive signal. Added HEALTH_UNKNOWN constant in policy_base; build_state_from_rows emits unknown when both telemetry signals are absent. is_eligible() excludes both unhealthy and unknown from routing — silent backends are no longer routed to. All policies (round_robin, least_connections, ppo) plus obs_builder.build_action_mask consume the same predicate. This release
Single forward pass + bounded fallback (M2, M4) PPOPolicy.act now does one forward pass (logit extraction; argmax derived from the masked logits). When every live backend is ineligible (unhealthy or unknown), the policy emits RoutingAction(mode=shadow, rankings=[]) instead of including the "best" ineligible one — the anomaly detector's existing LB-sidecar exclusions remain in effect rather than being undone by RL. This release
DB reconnect (M5) The run loop previously caught any DB exception, logged it, and kept the dead connection — the service stayed "up" but stopped publishing for the lifetime of the container. Now psycopg2.OperationalError is detected specifically; the loop closes the dead connection, opens a fresh one, logs DB connection rebuilt, and continues. Other DB exceptions (query bugs) still skip the cycle without reconnecting. This release
Case-insensitive action.mode (M7) effective_mode previously matched RL_MODE env case-insensitively but action mode case-sensitively. Both now lowercase before comparison. This release
Stable round-robin rotation (M8) RoundRobinPolicy previously kept a modular index _idx % len(eligible). When the eligible set shrunk (backend going degraded) the rotation reshuffled because the modular wrap-around point moved. Replaced with a backend_id pointer: the rotation advances to the lowest backend_id strictly greater than _last_id. Adding or removing a backend no longer perturbs the cycle. This release
"learning" operating_mode (M10) Previously accepted as a distinct value but identical to hybrid in all serving paths. Removed from the canonical set; policy_from_payload and PPOPolicy._normalise_operating_mode both transparently map learning → hybrid so existing policy.yaml files keep working. The canonical set is now {shadow, hybrid}. This release
Safety-boundary documentation (M3) The same intent (don't actually move traffic) appears in three places: runloop.effective_mode, lb-sidecar.handle_routing, and lb-sidecar.handle_policy. services/rl-engine/README.md now documents that the LB sidecar is the safety boundary; the rl-engine gate is telemetry hygiene. A future contributor "consolidating" the rl-engine gate would no longer remove unsafe-routing protection — the sidecar still refuses anything except mode=active. This release
Graceful shutdown signal (L4) Module-level _shutdown_event + SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers installed in __main__. The run loop polls the event between cycles; on shutdown it drains the current cycle, closes pubsub + DB, prints run loop exited. Docker's SIGTERM during compose down now produces a clean exit instead of leaving orphan pubsub subscriptions on Redis. This release
Test coverage Local pytest tests/unit/rl-engine/ count: 123 → 131 (gymnasium-dependent tests still skip locally). New tests cover the unknown-health state, learning → hybrid mapping, action.mode case insensitivity, anomaly-health TTL eviction, and the C4 reload-vs-bootstrap path. PPO test fixture-based suite updated for the empty-rankings contract and the argmax-dominant weight shape; runs in CI / sb3-contrib environment. This release

v1.0.3 — 2026-05-21 — Phase-2 SaaS scoping, slice machinery, anomaly-detector wired (#138 round 1)

AspectWhat changed / mergedResolved by
Forecast query unit fix — autoscaler chain verified end-to-end services/shared/queries.py::FORECAST_QUERY labelled its output column request_rate but returned SUM(value) over a fixed 1-minute bucket — i.e. requests-per-minute, not per-second. The moving-average engine then fed that number into ForecastResult.predicted_rps, and the autoscaler compared it to current_count × per_instance_capacity_rps for capacity math, so every forecast was inflated 60×. With FORECAST_RUNLOOP_ENABLED=true the autoscaler unconditionally requested scale-out, the request hit the compose cap (5 backends already running, no stopped containers to start), scale_out returned None per services/autoscaler/app.py:189, and the audit-skip branch fired silently — leaving the scaling_events table empty while logs filled with "cluster could not actuate". Surfaced live: at ~89 Locust RPS the forecaster predicted ~7500 RPS; per-minute bucket counts in TimescaleDB matched the Locust generation rate × 60 exactly. Fix is one line — divide SUM(value) by 60 in the SELECT — pinned by new test_forecast_query_normalises_to_per_second_rate in tests/integration/test_s2_baseline.py::TestCanonicalSQLParameterization. Verified post-fix: forecast tracks Locust within ~30% (acceptable MA noise) and the autoscaler emits noop when current capacity is sufficient. Audit-page surface unaffected — manual POST /api/v1/scale already populates it; the bug only blocked the auto-loop path. §8.6 Current state updated with the normalisation contract. This release

24. Historical & Superseded Designs

Canonical Design Intent

Earlier designs are not deleted. They are quarantined here so the canonical sections stay clean while the rationale for current choices remains traceable. Each entry records: what the alternative was, what was good about it, what was weak about it, and why the current canonical was preferred. Some entries are recommended for future revisit — they are not silently abandoned.

Historical: 10-sprint implementation-first plan (Implementation_Plan.pdf, Mar 2026)

What it was: A 10-sprint plan that implemented each module deeply (real ML, real models) before integrating them into the stack.

Why it was rejected: Advisor directive on 2026-04-28 (recorded in PLAN_CHANGELOG.md): "wire before you implement." The team was building isolated ML components that did not yet talk to each other; integration risk was accumulating.

Why something from it should be preserved: The depth of ML treatment per module (the per-engine subsections in the original plan) is still useful as scope material for §6 deep dives in this document.

Status: Superseded by 6-sprint integration-first plan (§17).

Historical: 8-sprint milestone plans (Milestone-Based Sprint Plan DOCXs)

What they were: Two parallel milestone-based plans authored as alternatives to the 10-sprint plan.

Why they were rejected: Same advisor directive. The integration-first reframing made both obsolete simultaneously.

Status: Historical. Kept for provenance only.

Draft (revisit later): V1 6-table schema (V1__smartload_schema.sql, May 4, 2026)

What it is: Richer 6-table schema with backend_nodes, policies, telemetry_metrics (explicit columns), routing_decisions, anomaly_events, scaling_events, plus 8 ENUM types, retention policies, continuous aggregates, and 7 days of seed data.

What it does well: Tighter type safety (ENUMs over TEXT), continuous aggregates for fast dashboards, explicit columns for the canonical metric set, retention policies baked in. Closer to a production schema.

What's weak today: Schema-rigid — every new metric requires ALTER TABLE. routing_decisions introduces a write source on or near the request path; placement needs care so the data plane stays pure. Migration from the canonical 3-table schema is not yet planned.

Why current canonical is preferred: The 3-table wide-narrow schema is wired and validated by 33 integration tests. Lower friction during the active research phase.

Recommendation: Revisit Phase 2 review. Once the metric set stabilizes, V1 becomes the strong target schema. Migration must include a backfill plan and re-validation of queries.py.

Draft (revisit later): SmartLoad Feature Contracts (Mar 27 DOCX)

What it is: Per-module ML feature contracts — input features, output schema, training schedule for each AI engine.

What it does well: Forces every engine to commit to its feature surface up front; would prevent feature creep.

What's weak today: Authored before integration-first reframing; assumes engines were further along than they are. Some features assume a richer schema (V1) that is not yet adopted.

Recommendation: Revisit alongside V1 schema. Adopt as a Phase 2 deliverable once V1 schema is reviewed.

Draft (revisit later): SmartLoad Service Contracts (Mar 28 DOCX) — SLA targets

What it is: Client / Backend / Operator SLAs including 99.5% availability, HTTP 207 multi-status semantics for partial backend failure, etc.

What it does well: Production-shaped commitments. Operationalizes principle 10 (prototype realism with production ambition).

What's weak today: 99.5% availability is not testable in the prototype harness. HTTP 207 semantics add complexity that the LB does not currently implement.

Recommendation: Confirm scope. Adopt SLO targets as canonical even if availability targets are aspirational. Defer HTTP 207 to a future iteration with explicit scope.

Historical: Go load balancer (Implementation_Plan.pdf primary; Node.js fallback)

What it was: The original tech-stack plan favored a Go-based load balancer (using net/http or Echo) for low-latency concurrency, with Node.js Express as a fallback.

Why it was rejected: NGINX is wired and battle-tested; integration risk + custom-LB risk outweighed the latency advantage at prototype scale.

What's still good about it: If SmartLoad ever needs L7 features beyond NGINX (custom routing rules, embedded sidecar logic), a Go LB is a credible target.

Status: Superseded by NGINX. Reconsider only if NGINX limits become the bottleneck.

Historical: Custom React + Flask operator UI

What it was: A custom web UI for operator dashboards and policy toggles, listed in SmartLoad_API_Design.pdf §11.5 "future extensions" and prototyped in mockups.

Why it was rejected (for now): Grafana already covers dashboarding. CLI/curl covers policy toggles. Building UI is scope expansion that doesn't move the research story forward.

Status: Future extension. Not on the critical path.

Historical: CloudSim / Mininet for RL simulation

What it was: External simulation environments for RL training (CloudSim for cloud workloads, Mininet for network effects).

Why it was rejected: A custom Python OpenAI Gym env (SmartLoadEnv) is lighter, faster, and easier to integrate with Stable-Baselines3. PLAN_CHANGELOG note: "lighter, faster custom Gym."

What's still good about CloudSim/Mininet: If RL needs to learn from realistic cloud-cost models or network jitter, those simulators have richer fidelity.

Status: Superseded by custom SmartLoadEnv.

Historical: Versioned Redis channels (smartload.<topic>.v1)

What it was: Versioned channel naming proposed in SmartLoad_API_Design.pdf.

Why it was deferred: The wired contracts.py uses unsuffixed channels; introducing .v1 now would be a breaking change with no current need.

Recommendation: Adopt .v1 the next time a contract genuinely breaks (rename or remove a field). Until then, keep unsuffixed.

Open design questions carried forward

  • Needs confirmation — Will V1 schema be adopted as a Phase 2 migration target?
  • Needs confirmation — Will Feature Contracts replace the lighter R0–R2 dataset plan, or coexist?
  • Needs confirmation — Sidecar language: Python (current default) vs Go.
  • Needs confirmation — Adopt Service Contracts SLA targets (99.5% availability, HTTP 207) as canonical operational commitments?
  • Needs confirmation — Migrate to .v1 envelopes once V1 schema is reviewed?

25. Distribution & Operating Surfaces Canonical

This section is the single canonical statement of how SmartLoad is delivered, how customers integrate, and where the operator-vs-tenant trust boundary sits. Added 2026-05-14 after the product was explicitly reframed from a thesis-bounded artefact to middleware + candidate SaaS. Everything else in this SOT remains accurate for the engineering view; §25 is the product view that the README and presentations route into.

Phase scope. SmartLoad ships today as single-tenant middleware. SaaS adaptation — multi-tenancy (#129), tenant API keys + RBAC (#132), rate limiting and per-token quotas (#135), per-tenant Redis namespacing, and the managed-SaaS delivery shape — is a separate, deferred Phase 2 track. Sections 25.1's Managed SaaS row, 25.3's Tenant integrator row, 25.4 in its entirety, and Figure 12.8 describe that future track. Nothing in current-phase scope should assume tenant_id plumbing, API-key auth, or per-tenant channel suffixes exist. The present build serves one integrating customer.

25.1 Three delivery shapes

ShapeWho runs itHow it shipsStatus
On-prem — Docker Compose Customer ops team docker compose up against the canonical docker-compose.yml at the repo root. Single-tenant. Shipped today
On-prem — Helm chart Customer ops team on Kubernetes helm install smartload ./infrastructure/helm/smartload. Single-tenant by default; multi-tenant by setting tenancy.enabled=true once #129 lands. Scaffolded, Chart.yaml/values.yaml present; templates pending issue #133
Managed SaaS SmartLoad operations team Same images, single shared control plane, per-tenant namespacing across DB, Redis, policy storage, API keys. Future; depends on #129 (multi-tenancy) and #132 (API keys + RBAC)

25.2 Customer integration surfaces

What an external system uses to plug into SmartLoad. Each surface has a canonical contract source; the SOT §11 endpoint tables become a human summary, not the contract.

SurfaceCanonical contract sourceSDK / toolingStatus
HTTP REST (/api/v1/*) docs/openapi/smartload-v1.yaml (OpenAPI 3.1) Swagger UI at http://<operator-ui>:8090/api/docs v1 covers policy + audit + telemetry + health
Redis pub/sub (control bus) docs/redis-channels.md SDK events.py helpers (Python today; Node / Go future) 5 channels registered; SDK subscribe_policy shipped slice #1
Webhook delivery (outbound HTTP) docs/openapi/smartload-v1.yaml (planned §11.3) HMAC-SHA256 signed; X-SmartLoad-Signature header; retry with exponential backoff Scaffolded; implementation tracked by #130
Python SDK clients/python/smartload_client/ pip install smartload-client (post-release); pip install -e clients/python for dev Policy methods shipped slice #1; other surfaces pending #127, #137
Operator UI services/operator-ui/ (BFF + web) Reachable at http://localhost:8090 against the live compose stack Home + Policy page shipped slice #1; other pages pending #121–#125, #131

25.3 Operator vs Tenant — trust boundary

SmartLoad has two distinct human roles whose privileges, surfaces, and audit trails must not overlap.

RoleIdentityWhat they can doAuthenticationAudit ledger
Operator Human inside the customer's ops team (self-host) or the SmartLoad ops team (SaaS) Edit policy, view audit, trigger scale/isolate (#123), view all engines Operator UI login (#125) policy_changes, scaling_events
Tenant integrator Phase 2 — SaaS (deferred) Service principal (machine-to-machine) Scoped subset: read policy + metrics, manage own webhooks, receive own events API key (#132) with role scopes: policy:read, policy:write, metrics:read, webhook:manage, scale:write, isolate:write, admin. Authentication is not enforced in the present middleware build — the SDK and HTTP API ship without auth until #132 lands as part of the SaaS adaptation track. Per-tenant API-key issue/revoke log (Phase 2)

The operator UI is not a tenant-facing surface. Tenants integrate via the SDK + webhooks + OpenAPI, not the UI. Per the SOT lock (commit 6f89a13) the UI is a transparency + override layer, not an admin panel.

25.4 Multi-tenancy namespacing pattern Phase 2 — SaaS adaptation (deferred)

This is forward-looking design, not present-phase scope. The current build is single-tenant. The table below documents how tenant_id would be plumbed when the SaaS adaptation track (#129) lands, so the eventual migration can be mechanical. Until that work begins, services must not require tenant_id, the Redis channels remain unsuffixed (smartload.<topic>), policy storage stays at config/policy.yaml, and no API surface requires an API key.

SurfaceNamespacing rule
TimescaleDB hypertablesEvery row carries tenant_id; indexed on (tenant_id, time). Migration plumbing tracked by #129.
Redis pub/sub channelst:<tenant_id>:smartload.<topic> for fan-out; unsuffixed smartload.<topic> remains as the single-tenant default during transition.
Policy storageconfig/tenants/<tenant_id>/policy.yaml. Today's config/policy.yaml is the default tenant.
HTTP requeststenant_id resolved from the API key (#132) or X-SmartLoad-Tenant header (admin path only).

25.5 LB adapter pattern — substitutability over coupling

SmartLoad is not "an NGINX product." The decision plane speaks to the LoadBalancerAdapter interface defined at services/shared/lb_adapters/base.py; only the nginx/ adapter is wired in v1, but envoy/, haproxy/, and alb/ folders exist as stubs so the import paths are real and the conformance contract (tests/conformance/lb_adapter/) is enforceable from day one.

25.6 Plugin-per-folder convention

Inside each AI service, replaceable strategies live one folder per plugin under engines/ or policies/:

  • services/anomaly-detector/engines/threshold/, engines/isolation_forest/
  • services/forecasting/engines/moving_average/, engines/arima/
  • services/rl-engine/policies/random_shadow/, policies/ppo/

Each plugin folder is self-contained: engine.py implementation, README.md describing rationale + fallback, and per-plugin test_engine.py. The factory in engine_base.py / policy_base.py selects the plugin from an env var; missing model files fall back to the baseline plugin without service restart. This is the design that lets a model author drop an isolation_forest.pkl into models/ and have the service swap engines with no code change in the service shell. Issue #138 wires the engine factory into the service run loop.

Engine bootstrap with safety-net fallback

At service startup, the run loop resolves the engine through a strict bootstrap sequence. If the requested engine fails to load — missing artifact, bad name, exception in __init__ — the service falls back to the named baseline (threshold / moving_average / random_shadow) and reports engine_ready=false on /health. The baseline itself is the safety net: if even it fails, startup raises, because that's a deployment bug and silence would mask it.

Run-loop cycle (per service, identical shape)

Once bootstrapped, each AI service runs a single thread that interleaves Redis pub/sub message handling and tick-based inference. The pubsub get_message uses a 1-second timeout so policy updates land within one second; between messages, the loop runs an inference cycle every POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS. Engine reconstruction on policy change keeps trained-model loading cheap (cached artifact) while honouring policy-derived constructor kwargs.

Model author handoff boundary

The diagrams above are the integration layer's contract. A model author owns only the boxed step — the engine class with its score() / forecast() / act() method, plus the trained artifact at services/<svc>/models/<name>.pkl. The Redis publish, the DB query, the policy reload, the fallback path, the health reporting, the unit-test scaffold — all owned by the integration layer. This is the boundary the PR #144 review codified.

As of 2026-05-21, all three AI services run the diagrams above: anomaly-detector (#138 round 1, threshold baseline), forecasting (round 2, moving-average baseline), and rl-engine (round 3, random-shadow baseline). The three RUNLOOP_ENABLED flags default to false on main; flipping them to true in the deployment env starts the run loops without code changes. Model handoffs (isolation_forest.pkl #101, arima.pkl #102, policy.zip #27) now have a stable integration target — drop the artifact + a thin engines/<name>/engine.py or policies/<name>/policy.py implementing the ABC, register the name in the factory, set <SVC>_ENGINE / RL_POLICY, done.

RL-specific extension: mode composition

The rl-engine adds one piece beyond the shared pattern: the published mode field on RoutingRecommendation is not the policy's own output. It's composed by runloop.effective_mode() from three inputs to keep RL output observable while denying it routing authority unless every gate agrees:

25.7 CI structural lints — the discipline that keeps the surfaces honest

ScriptInvariant
scripts/lint-structure.pyEvery services/<svc>/ has README; every plugin folder has README + test; every tests/e2e/<feature>/ has sibling docs/features/<feature>.md + examples/scenarios/<feature>/.
scripts/lint-redis-channels.pyEvery smartload.<topic> string in services/ appears in docs/redis-channels.md.
scripts/lint-openapi.pyEvery /api/v1/* route in services/ appears in docs/openapi/smartload-v1.yaml.

All three ship in permissive mode (warnings, exit 0). Issue #139 flips them to --strict in CI once the team has settled the convention. Without this enforcement, the structural decisions documented here would drift in the same way Istio's architecture/ docs drifted from its code — visible failure mode, deliberately avoided.

25.8 Feature manifest pattern

Every shippable feature has a single navigable home at docs/features/<feature>.md with a fixed shape: what the slice delivers, customer surfaces, implementation pointers, status checklist, how to verify, non-goals. The first manifest is policy-management.md (slice #1, 2026-05-14). Every feature follows this template; see docs/features/README.md for the canonical shape. The acceptance contract — what must be true for a slice to be called "done" — lives at docs/features/SLICE_CHECKLIST.md and is referenced from every manifest.

None of these rework existing architecture. Four are net-new endpoints / files; the HAProxy adapter slots into the LoadBalancerAdapter ABC already in place; the smartload.yml bootstrap is read-once + additive over the existing config files (which continue to work as the canonical runtime store). Architectural choices held: microservices decomposition over single main.py (per §25.6 figures, for failure isolation); TimescaleDB feature store over direct Prometheus reads in engines (for explainable ML reproducibility, see §14.x); per-engine RUNLOOP_ENABLED flag composition over a global control switch (for finer-grained operator pause / resume).

26. API Integration Guide Canonical

Canonical Design Intent

This section is the single navigable home for everyone who consumes SmartLoad over HTTP — operator tooling, customer middleware, the SDK, the operator UI BFF. It is the prose companion to docs/openapi/smartload-v1.yaml: the YAML is the machine-readable contract; this section explains what each endpoint means, how errors are shaped, how authentication will land, and how to choose between the four integration patterns.

26.1 Audience map — start here

I want to…Read this subsectionPlus
Call the API from a script in under 5 minutes§26.4 + §26.5§27 (SDK)
Build long-running middleware that reacts to events§26.4 + §26.9 (pattern C/D)§29 (Webhooks) or §27 §27.4 (Redis subscribe)
Understand the error model§26.5
Self-host and know what ports to expose§26.8§12.10 (K8s topology)
Plan for SaaS / multi-tenant adoption§26.3§12.8
Know what's stable and what may change§26.7

26.2 Authentication

ModeStatusHow a caller authenticates
Anonymous (today)ShippedNo headers required. Every endpoint is reachable from the compose network. The default tenant_id="default" is implicit. Single-tenant self-host only.
API key (planned)Issue #132HTTP header Authorization: Bearer sl_<key> or X-API-Key: sl_<key> (both accepted). Server resolves tenant_id from the key, then checks the scope matrix per endpoint.
Operator session (UI only)Issue #125Cookie-based session against the operator UI BFF; never reaches policy-manager directly. Out of scope for programmatic integration.

Until #132 lands: callers may still set Authorization / X-API-Key / X-Tenant-Id headers — they are ignored by the live service, but the SDK already populates them so integration code that sets them today will Just Work post-#132 with no caller changes.

API key scopes (planned)

ScopeEndpoints it unlocks
policy:readGET /api/v1/policy
policy:writePOST /api/v1/policy
metrics:readGET /api/v1/metrics, GET /api/v1/stats
webhook:managePOST/GET/DELETE /api/v1/webhooks
scale:writePOST /api/v1/scale (#123)
isolate:writePOST /api/v1/isolate (#123)
adminPOST/GET/DELETE /api/v1/api-keys; all of the above

26.3 Tenant scoping

Single-tenant deployments (today) map to the implicit tenant_id="default". Multi-tenant rollout (#129) plumbs tenant_id through every isolatable surface:

  • HTTP: derived from the API key. Admin paths may pass an explicit X-SmartLoad-Tenant header.
  • Redis: channels become t:<tenant_id>:smartload.<topic>. Subscribers filter on tenant.
  • Storage: every row in policy_changes, metrics, backend_health, scaling_events carries a tenant_id column indexed alongside time.
  • Policy file: config/tenants/<tenant_id>/policy.yaml (today flat config/policy.yaml = default tenant).

See §12.8 for the data flow diagram and §25.4 for the namespacing pattern.

26.4 Endpoint reference

The machine-readable source is docs/openapi/smartload-v1.yaml. Below is the human reference grouped by surface. Every endpoint follows the uniform error envelope from §26.5.

Policy (policy-manager, port 8086)

Method · PathPurposeBody / paramsSuccessErrors
GET /api/v1/policy Read the current operating policy — 200 + full Policy object 404 if policy.yaml is missing or empty
POST /api/v1/policy Propose and commit a policy change. Idempotent — POSTing the same body twice returns {status: "no-op"} the second time with no audit row, no envelope publish. Body: any subset of PolicyPatch. Headers: optional X-Actor (recorded in audit). 200 + PolicyUpdateResponse = {status, policy, changed_fields, policy_version, event_id} 400 + {error, field} on validation failure. 500 on persist failure (rare).
GET /api/v1/audit/policy Recent policy_changes rows, newest first. Query: ?limit=N (default 50, max 1000). 200 + array of PolicyAuditRow 400 on bad limit; 503 if TimescaleDB is unreachable
GET /health Uniform health endpoint — 200 + {status:"ok", redis:true, timescaledb:true, policy_loaded:bool, policy_version:int} 503 on degraded (SOT §11 — never 207)

Telemetry (telemetry, port 8081)

Method · PathPurposeBody / paramsSuccess
POST /v1/metrics OTLP/HTTP-JSON ingress (called by the OTel Collector — not an operator surface) OTLP body 200 (always — protocol contract)
GET /api/v1/metrics?service=<svc>&window=<dur> Recent metric rows for one service. window accepts 30s / 5m / 1h / 2d. Query: service (required), window (default 5m) 200 + array of MetricRow
GET /api/v1/stats Telemetry-service own observability counters (rows_written, batches_written, rows_dropped_db, rows_dropped_shape) — 200 + TelemetryStats
GET /health Uniform health — See policy-manager

Autoscaler (autoscaler, port 8085)

Method · PathPurposeStatus
GET /healthUniform health + currently loaded policy snapshotShipped
POST /api/v1/scaleManual override — request target backend countIssue #123
POST /api/v1/isolateManual override — drain traffic from a specific backendIssue #123

Operator UI BFF (operator-ui, port 8090)

The BFF is intended for browser consumption — programmatic integrators talk to policy-manager / autoscaler / telemetry directly. BFF endpoints under /api/ui/* are not part of /api/v1 and may change without a version bump.

Method · PathPurpose
GET /api/ui/healthFan-out aggregator: /health across every SmartLoad service, returned as a service-name → snapshot map
GET /api/ui/policyProxy to policy-manager GET /api/v1/policy
POST /api/ui/policyProxy to policy-manager POST /api/v1/policy (X-Actor passthrough)
GET /api/ui/audit/policyProxy to policy-manager GET /api/v1/audit/policy
GET /api/docsSwagger UI rendered from docs/openapi/smartload-v1.yaml
GET /api/openapi.yamlRaw spec (used by Swagger UI; consumable directly by tooling)

Webhook management (webhook-dispatcher — planned)

Spec lives in §29. Endpoints (POST/GET/DELETE /api/v1/webhooks) ship with issue #130.

26.5 Request / response idioms

Required and optional headers

HeaderDirectionRequired?Notes
Content-Type: application/jsonrequestPOST onlyBodies are JSON. Other types rejected with 415.
Authorization: Bearer sl_…requestfuture (#132)Today: ignored. Future: required on non-health endpoints.
X-API-Key: sl_…requestfuture (#132)Alternate to Authorization; both accepted.
X-Tenant-Id: <tenant>requestadmin only (#129)Future override; usually derived from the API key.
X-Actor: <name>requestrecommended on writesRecorded in audit rows. SDK sets smartload-client/<version> by default.
Retry-After: <sec>responseon 429Number of seconds to back off. Honour it.
Sunset: <date>responsefuture (#134)Returned on deprecated endpoints. Calls still succeed.
Deprecation: trueresponsefuture (#134)Paired with Sunset.

Error envelope

HTTP/1.1 400 Bad Request
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "error": "min_backends must be > 0 (got 0)",
  "field": "min_backends"
}

Every non-2xx response carries the Error schema: {error: string, field?: string}. The field property names the offending input when the server can pinpoint it (validation errors). The SDK maps these to typed exceptions — see §27 §27.5.

Status code matrix

CodeMeaningCaller behaviour
200 OKSuccess. POST returns 200 even on no-op (status: "no-op").Continue.
400 Bad RequestValidation failure. Body carries field.Fix the request; do not retry as-is.
401 UnauthorizedMissing / invalid API key (future).Refresh credentials.
403 ForbiddenAPI key valid but scope insufficient (future).Request additional scope.
404 Not FoundResource missing. /api/v1/policy returns this when policy.yaml is empty.POST a baseline policy first.
415 Unsupported Media TypePOST without application/json.Fix the header.
429 Too Many RequestsRate limit hit (future #135). Retry-After set.Sleep then retry.
500 Internal Server ErrorServer-side bug. Logged with traceback.Retry with backoff; file an issue if persistent.
503 Service UnavailableService or its upstream (Redis / TimescaleDB) is degraded. Returned by /health and /api/v1/audit/policy.Retry with backoff. K8s liveness probes treat as unhealthy.

SmartLoad does not use 207 Multi-Status. K8s liveness probes interpret 2xx as healthy and were classifying degraded services as healthy under the old 207 contract — this is fixed canonically (PR #105 in §22 v1.0.1).

Idempotency

POST /api/v1/policy is idempotent on body equality: posting the same patch twice produces one audit row, one envelope publish, and one policy_version bump. The second POST returns {status: "no-op", changed_fields: []}. Operators may safely retry without producing audit noise.

26.6 Rate limiting Issue #135

ScopeDefault limit
policy:write60 / minute per tenant
scale:write / isolate:write30 / minute per tenant
metrics:read600 / minute per tenant
webhook:manage30 / minute per tenant

Response headers on every limited endpoint (today and future): X-RateLimit-Remaining, X-RateLimit-Reset. 429 response carries Retry-After. Token-bucket algorithm; per-tenant quota overrides stored in tenant_quotas (admin only).

26.7 Versioning and deprecation Issue #134

  • Path versioning only. /api/v1 today; /api/v2 when a breaking change ships. No header- or query-string-based version negotiation.
  • Compatibility window: a deprecated version stays reachable for at least one calendar quarter after a successor ships.
  • Deprecation signal: every response from a deprecated endpoint carries Sunset: <date> and Deprecation: true.
  • Breaking change definition: removing a field, narrowing a type, renaming an enum value, changing default values, or moving a field from optional to required.
  • Non-breaking: adding a new optional response field, adding a new endpoint, adding a new optional request field.

26.8 Self-host port map

ServiceCompose portK8s ServiceName:Port (Helm default)Public-from-outside?
load-balancer (NGINX)8080load-balancer:80Yes — client traffic
telemetry8081telemetry:8081No — collector → service
anomaly-detector8082anomaly-detector:8082No — health only
forecasting8083forecasting:8083No — health only
rl-engine8084rl-engine:8084No — health only
autoscaler8085autoscaler:8085No — health + future scale/isolate
policy-manager8086policy-manager:8086Yes — programmatic integration target
operator-ui8090operator-ui:8090Yes — humans
locust (traffic simulator)8089not in HelmDev only
grafana3000via Prometheus stackOperator dashboards
prometheus9090via Prometheus OperatorOptional

For SaaS deployments, an ingress fronts operator-ui:8090 for humans and policy-manager:8086 (and future autoscaler public endpoints) for tenants. Everything else stays internal.

26.9 Integration patterns

Pick the pattern that matches what your integration does. Most middleware uses A or D.

PatternWhat it doesSurfaces usedExample
A — Read-only console Dashboard / status-page integration that polls SmartLoad for state. GET /api/v1/policy, GET /api/v1/audit/policy, GET /api/v1/metrics, GET /health Internal Slack /status command, Datadog widget
B — Synchronous operator Operator tooling that changes policy programmatically (CI flips safe_mode during deploys, etc.). Pattern A + POST /api/v1/policy Deploy pipeline that sets safe_mode=true during a backend rollout
C — Redis event listener (low-latency) Middleware that reacts to SmartLoad's decision events within seconds. Pattern A + Redis subscribe via SDK (subscribe_policy, future subscribe_anomaly etc.) Custom alerting that fires when a backend goes UNHEALTHY
D — Webhook consumer (no Redis) External system that cannot run a Redis subscriber; receives signed HTTP POSTs from webhook-dispatcher. Pattern A + POST /api/v1/webhooks registration (planned #130). See §29. Customer-side incident-management bot, audit-log shipper

26.10 Curl walkthrough — every shipped policy endpoint

# Read the current policy
curl -s http://localhost:8086/api/v1/policy | jq

# Flip safe_mode (idempotent)
curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8086/api/v1/policy \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -H 'X-Actor: ops-cli' \
  -d '{"safe_mode": true}' | jq

# Read the audit trail
curl -s 'http://localhost:8086/api/v1/audit/policy?limit=5' | jq

# Health (200 ok, 503 degraded)
curl -i http://localhost:8086/health

# Restore safe_mode
curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8086/api/v1/policy \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -H 'X-Actor: ops-cli' \
  -d '{"safe_mode": false}' | jq

26.11 OpenAPI spec + Swagger UI

The canonical machine-readable spec is docs/openapi/smartload-v1.yaml (OpenAPI 3.1). Mounted into the operator UI BFF as a read-only volume and rendered via Swagger UI at http://<operator-ui>:8090/api/docs. The scripts/lint-openapi.py guardrail enforces parity: every /api/v1/* route in source must appear in the spec.

27. SDK Reference (Python) Canonical

Canonical Design Intent

The smartload-client Python package is the supported way to integrate with SmartLoad programmatically. The SDK is version-locked to the HTTP and Redis contracts — when those evolve, the SDK ships in lockstep. Lives in the same repo as the services (anti-Temporal-multi-repo-coupling, §25). This section is the complete API reference; the source is clients/python/smartload_client/.

27.1 Installation

# Development (editable, from a clone of the SmartLoad repo)
pip install -e clients/python

# Production (after the first release; not yet on PyPI)
pip install smartload-client
DependencyVersionWhy
httpx≥ 0.27HTTP client (sync; connection pooling per client instance)
redis≥ 5.0Pub/sub subscriber; lazy-initialised on first subscribe_* call
Python≥ 3.10Type hints + match statements; modern dict/list generics

27.2 Constructor and configuration

from smartload_client import SmartLoadClient

client = SmartLoadClient(
    base_url             = "http://localhost:8086",   # policy-manager URL (slice #1 surface)
    autoscaler_url       = "http://localhost:8085",   # autoscaler URL — used by audit.scaling + actions.scale
    anomaly_detector_url = "http://localhost:8082",   # anomaly-detector URL — used by actions.isolate
    redis_url            = "redis://localhost:6379",  # Redis URL (lazy)
    api_key              = None,                      # bearer token (future #132)
    tenant_id            = "default",                 # tenant scope (future #129)
    default_actor        = "smartload-client",        # value sent in X-Actor on writes
    timeout              = 10.0,                      # request timeout (seconds)
    connect_timeout      = 3.0,                       # connect timeout (seconds)
)

The three URL parameters reflect SmartLoad's "owner of write is owner of read" discipline — policy surface lives on policy-manager, scaling surface on autoscaler, anomaly surface on anomaly-detector. In a gateway-backed deployment all three can point at the same URL.

Environment-variable fallbacks

ParameterEnv varDefault
base_url—http://localhost:8086
autoscaler_urlSMARTLOAD_AUTOSCALER_URLhttp://localhost:8085
anomaly_detector_urlSMARTLOAD_ANOMALY_DETECTOR_URLhttp://localhost:8082
redis_urlREDIS_URLredis://localhost:6379
api_keySMARTLOAD_API_KEYNone
tenant_idSMARTLOAD_TENANT_IDdefault

Context manager (recommended)

with SmartLoadClient(base_url="http://localhost:8086") as c:
    policy = c.get_policy()

# __exit__ closes httpx.Client and any lazily-created Redis client.
# Equivalent to calling c.close() explicitly.

27.3 Method reference

Each method is reachable two ways — via its sub-client (client.policy.get()) or via a top-level convenience (client.get_policy()). The two are equivalent; pick whichever reads better at the call site.

Surface at a glance

Sub-clientMethodsTop-level convenienceSlice
client.policyget(), update(patch, actor=), audit(limit=)get_policy(), set_policy(), audit_policy()#1 policy-management
client.eventssubscribe_policy(callback)subscribe_policy()#1
client.auditpolicy(limit=), scaling(limit=), list(kind, limit=)list_audit(kind, limit=)#2 audit-log
client.actionsscale(target_count, actor=, reason=), isolate(backend_id, status, actor=, reason=)scale(), isolate()#3 manual-actions
client.metricsread(service, window=)get_metrics()T1.1

SmartLoadClient.get_policy()

Returnsdict — full policy snapshot. Keys: operating_mode, safe_mode, min_backends, max_backends, slo_p95_latency_ms, anomaly_latency_multiplier, per_instance_capacity_rps, autoscaler_cooldown_seconds, policy_version, anomaly_response, anomaly_recovery_window_seconds, rl_exploration_rate, rl_confidence_threshold.
RaisesSmartLoadError (404 if policy.yaml empty), AuthenticationError (future), SmartLoadError on network failure.
Wire callGET /api/v1/policy

SmartLoadClient.set_policy(patch, *, actor=None)

Parameterspatch: dict — any subset of policy fields. actor: Optional[str] — recorded in audit; defaults to default_actor.
Returnsdict — {status, policy, changed_fields, policy_version, event_id}. status is "updated" on real change, "no-op" on idempotent retry.
RaisesValidationError with .field set on 400. AuthenticationError on 401/403. RateLimitError with .retry_after on 429. SmartLoadError on 5xx or network failure.
Wire callPOST /api/v1/policy with header X-Actor.
IdempotencyPosting the same patch twice produces one audit row, one envelope publish, and one policy_version bump. The second call returns {status: "no-op", changed_fields: []}.

SmartLoadClient.audit_policy(limit=50)

Parameterslimit: int — max rows (default 50, max 1000).
Returnslist[dict] — each row is {time, policy_version, field, old_value, new_value, actor}, newest first. old_value / new_value are JSON-decoded native values.
RaisesValidationError on bad limit. SmartLoadError on 503 if TimescaleDB is unreachable.
Wire callGET /api/v1/audit/policy?limit=N

SmartLoadClient.subscribe_policy(callback)

Parameterscallback: Callable[[dict, dict], None] — invoked with (payload, envelope_meta) for every PolicyUpdate envelope received on smartload.policy.
ReturnsPolicySubscription — a handle. Call .close() or use as a context manager to stop the background thread.
ThreadingRuns the callback on a daemon thread named smartload-policy-sub. Callback exceptions are caught + logged via logging.getLogger("smartload_client").exception; they never propagate or kill the thread.
StopCall sub.close() (sets a stop event, joins the thread up to 2 s, closes the underlying pubsub).

SmartLoadClient.list_audit(kind, limit=50) Slice #2 (#122)

Parameterskind: "policy" | "scaling" — selects the audit stream. limit: int — max rows (default 50, max 1000).
Returnslist[dict] — for kind="policy": same shape as audit_policy(). For kind="scaling": {time, action, instance_count, reason} per row, newest first. action ∈ {scale_out, scale_in, noop}.
RaisesValidationError(field="kind") on unknown kind. ValidationError(field="limit") on bad limit (server-side). SmartLoadError on 503 (audit datastore unreachable).
Wire callkind="policy" → GET base_url/api/v1/audit/policy?limit=N. kind="scaling" → GET autoscaler_url/api/v1/audit/scaling?limit=N. The two streams live on different upstream services; the SDK routes by kind.

SmartLoadClient.scale(target_count, *, actor=None, reason=None) Slice #3 (#123)

Parameterstarget_count: int — desired backend count, must satisfy policy.min_backends ≤ target ≤ policy.max_backends. actor, reason: Optional[str] — recorded in audit; defaults to default_actor + "manual override".
Returnsdict — {status, action, target_count, previous_count, final_count, steps_actuated, steps_requested, reason, event_id}. status is "applied" on real change or "noop" if target == current.
RaisesValidationError(field="target_count") on out-of-bounds / non-int. SmartLoadError on 503 (DB unreachable, cluster query failed).
Wire callPOST autoscaler_url/api/v1/scale
Side effectsCluster scaled step-by-step. ONE scaling_events row per call, prefixed manual:<actor>:. ONE ScalingEvent envelope with forecast_event_id=null. Cooldown clock bumped so auto-loop doesn't immediately undo.

SmartLoadClient.isolate(backend_id, status="unhealthy", *, actor=None, reason=None) Slice #3 (#123)

Parametersbackend_id: str — non-empty backend identifier. status: "healthy" | "degraded" | "unhealthy" — synthetic anomaly status. actor, reason: Optional[str].
Returnsdict — {status: "applied", backend_id, anomaly_status, score, actor, reason, event_id}. score is 0.0 for healthy, 1.0 for degraded/unhealthy.
RaisesValidationError(field="backend_id") on empty. ValidationError(field="status") on unknown status.
Wire callPOST anomaly_detector_url/api/v1/isolate
Side effectsSynthetic AnomalyEvent published on smartload.anomaly. ONE backend_health row written. model_version tagged "manual:<actor>" so subscribers can distinguish operator overrides from engine output.

Surface stubs (raise NotImplementedError)

The following methods are reachable but not yet wired. Each raises NotImplementedError with a pointer to the issue that lands the real implementation.

MethodTracking issue
subscribe_anomaly(cb) / subscribe_forecast / subscribe_routing / subscribe_scale#127 (full SDK event subscriptions)
client.webhooks.register(url, events, secret)#130
client.webhooks.list() / unregister(id)#130

27.4 Event subscriptions — model and lifecycle

import threading

with SmartLoadClient(base_url="http://localhost:8086") as c:
    received = []
    arrived  = threading.Event()

    def on_update(payload, meta):
        received.append(payload)
        arrived.set()

    sub = c.subscribe_policy(on_update)
    try:
        # Drain pre-attach backlog if relevant (cooperating tests).
        # The SDK does not auto-drain because the right policy is
        # caller-specific.
        c.set_policy({"safe_mode": True}, actor="my-tool")
        arrived.wait(timeout=5.0)
        print("envelope received:", received[-1]["policy_version"])
    finally:
        sub.close()
  • Channel TTL: the SDK applies the canonical per-channel TTL (§11). Stale messages are dropped before the callback runs.
  • Redis disconnect: if the underlying socket drops, the SDK logs and retries with a 1 s backoff. For long-running subscribers, monitor the smartload_client logger.
  • Threading model: one daemon thread per subscribe_* call. Several active subscriptions are fine; close them when done.
  • Backlog before attach: pub/sub is not durable. Messages published before subscribe_policy is called are not delivered.

27.5 Exception types

ExceptionRaised whenUseful attribute
SmartLoadErrorBase class; raised on 5xx, network errors, or anything not mapped below.Standard str(exc)
ValidationErrorHTTP 400 — body validation failed..field: str | None — names the offending input.
AuthenticationErrorHTTP 401 / 403 — credentials missing, invalid, revoked, or insufficient scope.—
RateLimitErrorHTTP 429 — rate limit hit (future #135)..retry_after: int | None — seconds parsed from Retry-After header.

All four inherit from SmartLoadError, so except SmartLoadError catches everything the SDK throws. Catch specific subtypes when you can recover differently (e.g., back off on RateLimitError).

27.6 Examples — by integration pattern

Quickstart — 20-line read

from smartload_client import SmartLoadClient, SmartLoadError

with SmartLoadClient(base_url="http://localhost:8086") as c:
    try:
        p = c.get_policy()
    except SmartLoadError as exc:
        print("could not read policy:", exc)
        raise
    print(p["operating_mode"], p["safe_mode"], p["policy_version"])

Source: clients/python/examples/quickstart.py.

Pattern B — synchronous operator (flip safe_mode for a deploy)

with SmartLoadClient(base_url="http://localhost:8086") as c:
    # Snapshot for restore
    baseline = c.get_policy()
    c.set_policy({"safe_mode": True}, actor="deploy-pipeline")

    # ... run the deploy ...

    c.set_policy({"safe_mode": baseline["safe_mode"]},
                 actor="deploy-pipeline-restore")

Pattern C — Redis event listener (full slice walk)

The runnable end-to-end script for the policy-management slice: examples/scenarios/policy-management/policy_walk.py. It snapshots policy, subscribes to smartload.policy, toggles a field, waits for the envelope, restores, and prints the audit trail. Used as the canonical demo + the e2e test driver.

Pattern D — Webhook consumer

Use the webhooks management methods (planned, §29) to register URLs. The SDK does not implement webhook receiver logic — your application's HTTP server handles incoming POSTs. The SDK provides the registration surface only.

27.9 Testing the SDK

Unit tests cover the status-code → exception mapping and the envelope parser. The e2e suite proves the SDK end-to-end against the live stack — it doubles as the slice's customer-surface acceptance.

28. Operator UI Guide Canonical

Canonical Design Intent

The operator UI is a transparency + override layer (per SOT lock 6f89a13), not an admin panel. A human operator opens it to see what every engine is currently doing, change policy with a diff preview, and trigger manual scale / isolate actions. It is not a tenant-facing surface — integrators use the SDK and webhooks. This section is the complete user + architecture reference for the UI shipped in vertical slice #1.

28.1 Architecture — two components, one image

ComponentTechRole
services/operator-ui/bff/ Flask + gunicorn + httpx + flask-swagger-ui Backend-for-frontend. Aggregates /health across services, proxies /api/ui/* to upstream (policy-manager etc.), mounts Swagger UI at /api/docs, serves the built SPA at /. Listens on port 8090.
services/operator-ui/web/ React 18 + Vite + TypeScript + react-diff-viewer-continued + react-router-dom Single-page app. Four pages today (Home + Policy + Audit + Actions); Live Engines (#121), Embedded metrics (#131), Logs (#124) pending. Talks to the BFF via /api/ui/*. Direct URLs and hard refreshes work for every page (BFF's static handler scoped to /assets/* so the SPA fallback always wins).

The two ship as one Docker image: a multi-stage Dockerfile builds the Vite app in a Node 20 stage then copies dist/ into a Python 3.11 slim runtime that runs the BFF. The runtime image carries no node_modules and no build tools.

28.2 What the operator sees

Home page (path /)

PurposeOne-screen overview of every SmartLoad service's health.
PollingFetches /api/ui/health every 10 seconds.
LayoutService-health grid — one pill per service, coloured by status.
Per-service pillName · status (ok / degraded / unreachable) · HTTP code · redis/timescaledb booleans · any error message.
Sourceservices/operator-ui/web/src/pages/Home.tsx

Policy page (path /policy)

PurposeRead, edit, diff-preview, commit operating policy; see recent audit rows.
PollingRefreshes current policy + audit every 10 seconds.
LayoutFour cards top-to-bottom — Current policy (read-only), Edit (JSON textarea), Diff preview (side-by-side), Recent audit (table).
Sourceservices/operator-ui/web/src/pages/Policy.tsx
Policy editor workflow
  1. Read: page mount fetches GET /api/ui/policy. Top card renders the response as formatted JSON. Editor textarea is pre-filled with the same content.
  2. Edit: operator modifies the textarea. Live validation: JSON.parse on every keystroke; invalid JSON disables the Commit button and shows the parser error inline.
  3. Diff: the side-by-side diff viewer (react-diff-viewer-continued, dark theme) renders current vs draft. Updates as the operator types.
  4. Commit: button calls POST /api/ui/policy. policy_version is stripped from the body (server-managed). Header X-Actor: operator-ui attached. On 2xx, a toast confirms — green for updated, neutral for no-op. On 4xx, toast carries error + field.
  5. Audit: bottom card re-fetches and displays the latest 20 rows. The operator sees their own change land at the top within one refresh tick.
  6. Reset: dedicated button — discards the draft and restores the textarea to the on-disk policy.
Diff preview semantics
  • Compares current JSON (left) against the parsed draft (right).
  • If the draft is invalid JSON, the right side renders (invalid JSON) and the parser error appears next to the Commit button (which is disabled).
  • Added / removed / changed fields are highlighted line-by-line. The viewer is purely textual — no semantic awareness of field types.
  • If the operator manually adds a policy_version to the draft, it is silently stripped on commit (the server bumps it).

Audit page (path /audit) Slice #2 (#122)

PurposeUnified viewer over both audit streams — policy_changes (from policy-manager) and scaling_events (from autoscaler).
PollingAuto-refreshes every 10 s; manual Refresh button forces an immediate poll.
LayoutToolbar (Kind chips · actor/field text filter · Action chips · Limit selector · Refresh) + two tables (Policy changes top, Scaling events bottom). Each table can be hidden via the Kind chips.
Filters (client-side)Kind: All / Policy / Scaling. Actor/field: substring match. Action: All / scale_out / scale_in. Limit: 25 / 50 / 100 / 250 / 500.
Sourceservices/operator-ui/web/src/pages/Audit.tsx

Actions page (path /actions) Slice #3 (#123)

PurposeOperator override surface — scale to N backends, isolate a backend. Force route weights is a placeholder until T2.1 sidecar (#82) lands.
HeaderFree-text actor input (placeholder until OUI.7 auth) + live policy bounds + current policy_version.
Confirmation flowEach Submit button opens a confirmation modal that previews the exact state change ("This will adjust the running backend count to N immediately. Audit row will be written with actor=<X>.") before any POST fires. Continue / Cancel — backdrop click cancels.
Results feedLatest 10 actions rendered as a table with colour-coded action badges. Cross-link to /audit for the persistent view.
Sourceservices/operator-ui/web/src/pages/Actions.tsx

Live Engines page (path /engines) #121 session 1

PurposeTransparency window into the decision plane — per-engine state + sub-second feed of every control-bus envelope. The page where an operator looks during an incident to see what the AI services are actually deciding.
LayoutTwo-pane (1.4 fr engine tiles · 1 fr activity feed). Collapses to a single column under 980 px. Stream-state indicator (● live / ○ connecting / ✕ error) lives in the page header.
Engine tile (left)One tile per AI service (anomaly-detector, forecasting, rl-engine). Each leads with a service-specific headline — anomaly tile shows non-healthy backends inline ("⚠ 2 of 4 unhealthy · b1 degraded@0.92 …"); forecasting tile shows predicted rps + CI band; rl-engine tile shows mode + top-3 rankings. Below the headline: secondary line with engine name + channel + (rl) rl_mode_env. Footer chips for safe_mode / fallback ← X / not ready. Tile status (ok/warn/bad) sets a coloured left border. A Details ▸ button opens a right-slide drawer with the full counters, policy snapshot, and raw last_output JSON — keeps the tile breathable while preserving access to the forensics.
Activity feed (right)Live envelope stream sourced from /api/ui/engines/stream via EventSource. One row per event, colour-coded by channel via a 3 px left border (anomaly red / forecast blue / routing purple / scale green). Channel filter chips at the top (All / anomaly / forecast / routing / scale). Newest-first. FEED_MAX=200 client-side cap so the DOM stays bounded during sustained traffic. Replay-on-connect (the BFF sends the current ring contents first) so a freshly-opened page isn't blank.
Polling cadenceEngine tiles re-fetch /api/ui/engines/snapshot every 5 s. The activity feed is push-only via SSE — no polling.
Sourceservices/operator-ui/web/src/pages/LiveEngines.tsx (page) · services/operator-ui/bff/engines.py (BFF ring buffer + event bus + subscriber loop).
BFF data flow

The operator-UI BFF process holds the entire Live Engines state machine; the AI services are unaware that a UI exists.

  1. One daemon thread per process (lazy-started on first /api/ui/engines/* request) subscribes to smartload.{anomaly,forecast,routing,scale} via the canonical shared.contracts.subscribe_envelope helper. Stale messages (per channel TTL — anomaly/routing 30 s, forecast 180 s, scale never) and malformed envelopes are dropped at the parser; valid ones become the canonical entry shape {channel, envelope:{event_id,source,version,timestamp}, payload}.
  2. Each entry is appended to a per-channel collections.deque(maxlen=100) (the ring buffer) and broadcast to every connected SSE subscriber's bounded queue.Queue(maxsize=256).
  3. A slow SSE client whose queue is full has events silently dropped for that client — the ring buffer still has them; back-pressure to the publisher would compromise the design. The subscriber loop never blocks.
  4. On Redis disconnect, the loop catches the exception, sleeps the reconnect delay, and rebuilds the subscription. The ring is preserved (in-process state); a BFF restart rebuilds the ring from the next message — no persistence required.
  5. Gunicorn worker class is gthread (2 workers × 8 threads) so a long-lived SSE connection occupies one thread, not one whole worker.

28.3 Authentication and authorization

AspectTodayFuture
Operator loginNone enforced — anyone on the host can reach :8090Session-based login (#125)
BFF-to-upstream authNone (all calls anonymous)BFF holds a service-account API key; passes X-Actor from the session (#125 + #132)
Browser-to-BFF CSRFNone enforcedStandard CSRF token + Same-Site cookie (#125)
Tenant scopingSingle tenant (default)Operator's session resolves which tenant the UI is scoped to (#129)

Until #125 lands, deploy the operator UI behind an existing trusted boundary (VPN, mTLS gateway, single-sign-on proxy) — it is not safe to expose :8090 directly to the internet.

28.4 BFF endpoint reference

BFF endpoints live under /api/ui/*. They are not part of the public /api/v1 contract and may change without a version bump. Programmatic integrators talk to policy-manager directly via /api/v1/*.

Method · PathBehaviour
GET /api/ui/healthFan-out via ThreadPoolExecutor to /health on every upstream service (3 s/call). Returns {all_ok: bool, services: {name: {status, status_code, redis, timescaledb, error?, extra}}}.
GET /api/ui/policyProxy → policy-manager:8086/api/v1/policy. Body passthrough.
POST /api/ui/policyProxy → policy-manager:8086/api/v1/policy. Content-Type: application/json. X-Actor is read from incoming header, defaulting to operator-ui when absent.
GET /api/ui/audit/policyProxy → policy-manager:8086/api/v1/audit/policy. Forwards ?limit.
GET /api/ui/audit/scaling Slice #2Proxy → autoscaler:8085/api/v1/audit/scaling. Forwards ?limit. Different upstream than the policy audit; the BFF normalises the two origins so the Audit page hits one URL space.
POST /api/ui/scale Slice #3Proxy → autoscaler:8085/api/v1/scale. Body passthrough; injects X-Actor: operator-ui when caller omits it.
POST /api/ui/isolate Slice #3Proxy → anomaly-detector:8082/api/v1/isolate. Body passthrough; injects X-Actor: operator-ui when caller omits it.
GET /api/ui/engines/snapshot #121 session 1Parallel fan-out to each AI service's /api/v1/engine/state plus a point-in-time view of the BFF's ring buffer. Body: {services:{name:body|{reachable:false,error}}, channels:{ch:[entries]}, recent:[entries]}. First call lazy-starts the daemon Redis-subscriber thread.
GET /api/ui/engines/stream #121 session 1SSE — replay-then-live. On connect the BFF emits the current ring buffer (newest-last), then forwards every new envelope. Heartbeat comment (: heartbeat\n\n) every 15 s so idle proxies don't close the connection. Headers: Cache-Control: no-cache, X-Accel-Buffering: no, Connection: keep-alive. Each client gets a bounded queue.Queue(maxsize=256); slow clients drop events silently. Connection lifecycle unsubscribes in the generator's finally.
GET /api/docsSwagger UI rendered from the canonical OpenAPI spec.
GET /api/openapi.yamlRaw spec from /app/openapi/smartload-v1.yaml (mounted as a read-only Docker volume).
GET /assets/*Static Vite-built JS / CSS bundles. Flask's static_url_path is scoped to /assets (not "") so the SPA fallback below catches every other path — direct URLs to /policy, /audit, /actions all render correctly on hard refresh.
GET /healthBFF's own health endpoint.
GET /, GET /<anything>SPA fallback — serves web/dist/index.html so the React router can pick up the path.

28.6 Page roadmap

OUI.8 Embedded charts (no Grafana context switch)#131Future
OUI.7 Operator UI authentication#125Phase 2 SaaS (deferred per §25 scope)

28.7 Design choices — what we explicitly didn't do

  • No iframe-Grafana. Charts are first-class UI elements (planned #131), not embedded Grafana dashboards. Grafana stays for SRE-grade ad-hoc PromQL exploration — the operator UI is for transparency and override during incidents.
  • No backend management surface (delete tenant, edit user). The UI is operator-facing, not admin-facing. SaaS-side admin tooling is a separate concern outside this SOT.
  • No client-side caching beyond the React state. Auto-refresh is the correctness model — the operator should always see fresh state during an incident.
  • SSE, not WebSocket, for the live transport. Slices #1–#3 used 10 s polling — sufficient for state that changes on operator action. The Live Engines page (#121) needs sub-second updates as envelopes land on the control bus, so it uses Server-Sent Events: one-way, Flask-native, browsers reconnect automatically. WebSocket was deliberately rejected — bidirectional capability for no client-to-server traffic, and an additional dependency.
  • BFF in-memory ring buffer, not a dedicated archiver service. The original #121 design proposed a separate services/events-archiver/ that would LPUSH envelopes into a Redis smartload:events:recent list capped at 1000 via LTRIM. The shipped design keeps the buffer inside the operator-UI BFF process (per-channel collections.deque(maxlen=100)), preserving §28's "pure read, not a SmartLoad layer" framing — engines stay unaware that a UI exists, and a BFF restart rebuilds the buffer from the next message. A durable events_log hypertable in TimescaleDB remains a deliberate Sprint 5+ stretch (see §28.5 Gap to close); the in-memory buffer is sufficient for the operator-visibility use case the page actually serves.

28.8 Running the UI

# Production-equivalent (whole stack)
docker compose up -d
# open http://localhost:8090/

# Dev with hot-reload (frontend only)
cd services/operator-ui/web
npm install
npm run dev          # Vite on :5173; proxies /api/ui to localhost:8090
# in another terminal:
cd ../bff
pip install -r requirements.txt
python app.py        # BFF on :8090

29. Webhooks Specification Canonical (target) Issue #130

Canonical Design Intent

Webhooks are the universal middleware contract for event delivery — every external system can receive HTTP POSTs, but not every external system can run a Redis subscriber. SmartLoad's webhook-dispatcher service consumes the canonical Redis channels (§11) and fans them out to customer-registered URLs with HMAC signing and retry-with-backoff. This section is the complete spec — registration, delivery semantics, signing algorithm, retry policy, customer-side verification. Implementation tracked by #130.

29.1 Why webhooks (vs Redis subscribe vs polling)

ChannelBest whenLimitation
Direct Redis subscribe (SDK subscribe_*)You can run Redis client code, sub-second latency matters, you're inside the same network as SmartLoadCustomer must speak Redis protocol; pub/sub is not durable, missed messages are gone
Webhooks (this section)You speak HTTP, you can host a public endpoint, sub-30-second latency is fine, you want at-least-once deliveryUp to 5 retry attempts; eventual consistency; latency in seconds
Polling RESTRead-only state inspection (current policy, audit history); you can't host an inbound endpointNo event push; you only see state at GET time, not transitions

Most external integrators land on webhooks. The SDK can register them programmatically; the operator UI will surface a webhook-management page (planned).

29.2 Registration lifecycle

Register a webhook

POST /api/v1/webhooks
Authorization: Bearer sl_<key>            # admin or webhook:manage scope (#132)
Content-Type: application/json

{
  "url":     "https://customer.example.com/smartload-events",
  "events":  ["anomaly", "scale", "policy"],          // subset of catalog
  "secret":  "wh_secret_",          // HMAC key
  "description": "incident-bot pager"                  // optional
}
FieldRequired?Notes
urlyesHTTPS strongly recommended; HTTP is accepted for dev but warned
eventsyesNon-empty array. Each value is one of anomaly / forecast / routing / scale / policy (catalog in §29.3)
secretyes≥ 32 chars. Stored hashed server-side. Used by both ends to compute and verify HMAC.
descriptionnoFree-form label for the operator UI dead-letter view
Response (201 Created)
{
  "id": "wh_8c91…",
  "tenant_id": "default",
  "url": "https://customer.example.com/smartload-events",
  "events": ["anomaly", "scale", "policy"],
  "created_at": "2026-05-14T12:00:00Z",
  "status": "active"
}

List, inspect, delete

Method · PathPurpose
GET /api/v1/webhooksList all webhooks for the authenticated tenant. Returns array of webhook records (no secret).
GET /api/v1/webhooks/{id}Inspect one webhook + recent delivery counters.
DELETE /api/v1/webhooks/{id}Unregister. Idempotent — returns 204 No Content whether or not the webhook existed.
POST /api/v1/webhooks/{id}/testSend a synthetic webhook.test event to the URL. Useful for verifying the customer endpoint is reachable + signs correctly. Result returned synchronously.

Lifecycle states

StateMeaningTransitions
activeReceiving deliveries→ suspended (auto, after sustained failures); ← suspended (admin re-enable)
suspendedDeliveries paused — too many consecutive failures (50 in a row in 1 h)→ active via PATCH (manual re-enable); → deleted via DELETE

29.3 Event catalog

Every webhook payload is a JSON object with two top-level keys: envelope (the canonical envelope from §11) and data (the channel-specific payload). The wrapper makes signature verification simpler — sign the whole JSON body, the customer parses it once.

Event typeUnderlying channelFrequencydata shape
anomaly smartload.anomaly Every 5 s per backend (only if status changes — coalesced) {backend_id, status, score, timestamp, features?, model_version?}
forecast smartload.forecast Every 60 s {horizon_minutes, predicted_rps, confidence_lower, confidence_upper, timestamp, model_id?}
routing smartload.routing Every 5 s (coalesced — only on rank change) {mode, server_rankings:[{backend_id, score}], timestamp, policy_version?}
scale smartload.scale On scale action only {action, instance_count, reason, timestamp, forecast_event_id?}
policy smartload.policy On policy change only Full policy snapshot + policy_version + changed_fields

Event types routing and anomaly are coalesced on the dispatcher side — a repeated identical message within 30 s is suppressed. Customers receive transitions, not heartbeats.

Sample delivery — anomaly

POST https://customer.example.com/smartload-events
Content-Type: application/json
X-SmartLoad-Event: anomaly
X-SmartLoad-Event-Id: a9f2…
X-SmartLoad-Webhook-Id: wh_8c91…
X-SmartLoad-Signature: t=1715683200,v1=4f1d8c5e6a3b…
X-SmartLoad-Delivery-Attempt: 1

{
  "envelope": {
    "event_id": "a9f2…",
    "source": "anomaly-detector",
    "version": 1,
    "timestamp": "2026-05-14T12:00:00.123Z"
  },
  "data": {
    "backend_id": "test-backend-3",
    "status": "unhealthy",
    "score": 0.92,
    "timestamp": "2026-05-14T12:00:00.000Z"
  }
}

29.4 HMAC signing

Algorithm — HMAC-SHA256 with timestamp prefix

Modelled on Stripe's webhook signing scheme. Resists replay attacks via a timestamp inside the signed payload; resists tampering via the body hash. Customer rejects requests where the timestamp differs from now by more than a tolerance (suggested: 5 minutes).

signed_payload = "{unix_timestamp}.{raw_request_body}"
signature      = hmac_sha256(secret, signed_payload).hex()
header         = f"t={unix_timestamp},v1={signature}"
# sent as:        X-SmartLoad-Signature: t=…,v1=…
ElementNotes
unix_timestampInteger seconds, set by the dispatcher at send time. Refreshed on every retry.
raw_request_bodyExact UTF-8 byte body, no re-serialisation. The customer MUST verify against the bytes they received, not against a re-parsed JSON.
Header formatComma-separated k=v pairs. Future scheme versions add v2=… alongside v1=… for graceful rotation.
Hex caseLowercase hex. Customers may upper-case before constant-time compare.

Verification — Python

import hmac, hashlib, time

TOLERANCE_SECONDS = 300  # 5 minutes

def verify_smartload_signature(raw_body: bytes, header: str, secret: str) -> bool:
    parts = dict(p.split("=", 1) for p in header.split(","))
    t   = int(parts["t"])
    sig = parts["v1"]

    if abs(time.time() - t) > TOLERANCE_SECONDS:
        return False  # too old → reject (replay defence)

    signed_payload = f"{t}.".encode() + raw_body
    expected = hmac.new(secret.encode(), signed_payload, hashlib.sha256).hexdigest()
    return hmac.compare_digest(expected, sig)

Verification — Node.js

import crypto from "crypto";

const TOLERANCE_SECONDS = 300;

export function verifySmartLoadSignature(rawBody, header, secret) {
  const parts = Object.fromEntries(header.split(",").map(p => p.split("=", 2)));
  const t   = parseInt(parts.t, 10);
  const sig = parts.v1;

  if (Math.abs(Date.now() / 1000 - t) > TOLERANCE_SECONDS) return false;

  const signedPayload = Buffer.concat([Buffer.from(`${t}.`), rawBody]);
  const expected      = crypto.createHmac("sha256", secret).update(signedPayload).digest("hex");
  return crypto.timingSafeEqual(Buffer.from(expected, "hex"), Buffer.from(sig, "hex"));
}

29.5 Delivery + retry policy

AspectSetting
Connect timeout10 s
Read timeout30 s
Success criterionCustomer responds 2xx within the read timeout
Retry triggersCustomer returns 5xx · network error · timeout
No-retry triggersCustomer returns 4xx (other than 408 / 425 / 429) — interpreted as "your request is bad, don't bother retrying"
Retry scheduleExponential backoff with jitter — attempt 1 immediate, then ~30 s, ~2 m, ~10 m, ~30 m (5 attempts max, ~45 m total window)
Header on retriesX-SmartLoad-Delivery-Attempt: N (1-indexed)
Signature on retriesRecomputed each attempt with the current timestamp — customer's tolerance window must accommodate the latest send, not the original.

Final-failure path

  1. Final attempt fails → row written to webhook_delivery_failures.
  2. Failure counter webhook_delivery_failures_total{webhook_id, event_type} incremented (Prometheus).
  3. 50 consecutive failures within 1 h → webhook auto-suspended (state transitions to suspended). The operator must manually re-enable.
  4. Operator UI dead-letter view (planned with #122) renders the latest N failures per webhook with the response body / status for debugging.

At-least-once semantics

Customers MUST be idempotent on receipt. The same X-SmartLoad-Event-Id may arrive more than once if a retry slips past a successful-but-slow customer response. Persist the event-id in a dedup table with a short TTL (≥ 1 h).

29.6 Security considerations

  • Secret confidentiality: the secret is delivered once at registration time (in the response body). It's hashed before storage. If lost, the customer must rotate by deleting + re-registering — there is no "fetch existing secret" surface.
  • Secret rotation: the v1 signature header supports adding v2=… in the future. Customers verify against any present version; the dispatcher emits whichever is configured. Migration path: dispatcher emits both v1 and v2 for a window; customer updates verifier; dispatcher drops v1.
  • HTTPS endpoint: required for any production webhook. The dispatcher logs (and the UI surfaces) a "non-HTTPS endpoint" warning on registration.
  • IP allow-list (future): customers can request a static egress IP for SmartLoad-side outbound deliveries when the SaaS control plane lands. Not in scope for self-host.
  • Replay defence: 5-minute timestamp tolerance. Customers tighten if their integration allows; loosen only when retrying across long network partitions.
  • Body re-serialisation pitfall: customers must verify against the raw body bytes, not against a JSON re-serialisation. Any whitespace or key-ordering change breaks the hash.

29.7 Tenant + multi-tenancy

Webhook records carry tenant_id — a tenant cannot register webhooks for another tenant's events. After multi-tenancy lands (#129), the dispatcher consumes from t:<tenant_id>:smartload.<topic> per tenant; today (single-tenant), it consumes from the unsuffixed channels.

29.8 Implementation pointers

WhereWhat
services/webhook-dispatcher/The service itself — subscribes to Redis, signs, POSTs, retries. Not yet implemented (#130); placeholder doc lives at docs/planned/webhook-dispatcher.md until the service folder is created.
services/policy-manager/app.py (planned)Webhook management endpoints — the policy-manager hosts them today; may move to a dedicated webhook-manager service if scope expands.
docs/openapi/smartload-v1.yaml (planned)Endpoint specs land here as #130 ships.
tests/e2e/webhook-delivery/ (planned)End-to-end suite: register URL → publish event → assert customer endpoint received within 5 s with valid signature.
examples/scenarios/webhook-delivery/ (planned)Runnable demo: register a webhook against a local echo server, publish a synthetic anomaly, observe delivery.

The reader should not have to triangulate four other sections to answer a database question.

30.1 Storage layer at a glance

StoreRoleSource of truth
TimescaleDB (PostgreSQL 16 + TimescaleDB extension)Durable time-series + audit storageinfrastructure/timescaledb/init.sql

Filesystemconfig/policy.yaml (operating policy, mounted into services that read it)policy-manager owns writes via POST /api/v1/policy

Nothing else is durable. Service container disks are ephemeral; the only state that survives a docker compose down is the timescaledb-data named volume + config/policy.yaml on the host.
